- **arsdlc-brainstorm**: Front door for any new request, before creative or engineering work. Use when the user says "I have an idea", "let's build", "brainstorm", "options", "how should we approach", or gives a vague one-line ask. Classifies the work into a lane (spike, quick, standard, regulated) so process matches stakes, compares 2-3 approaches, applies the ponytail ladder, and gets human approval before routing. Never writes code. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-brainstorm) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-brownfield**: Safe entry into an existing codebase. Produces a baseline, golden-master/characterization tests and named seams before any delta-only change is allowed. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-brownfield) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-craft**: Bridge to code-craft, the line-level clean-code discipline. Use during BUILD and review, before writing or refactoring code, over anything: over-engineering, dead code, deep nesting, any/casts, swallowed errors, duplication. Prefers the official code-craft skill (AnshRoshan/ansh-other-skills) when installed, falls back to an embedded distillation, and adds the arsdlc scope rules - craft serves the spec, touched lines only. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-craft) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-debug**: Evidence-backed debugging loop for bugs, flaky tests, regressions and "works on my machine". Use when the user says "bug", "broken", "fix this", "why does", "crash", "flaky", "slow", pastes a stack trace, or verification finds a failing scenario. Reproduce first, build a loop that goes red on the exact symptom, minimise, hypothesise, instrument, fix the root cause not the symptom, add a regression test, record every run. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-debug) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-delta**: Any change of intent after the spec is signed - new requirement, changed behaviour, removed scope, or a bug that reveals the spec was wrong. Use when the user says "actually", "change of plan", "can we also", "that's not what I meant", or implementation meets behaviour the spec does not cover. Writes a delta file, updates the spec explicitly, and invalidates stale approvals on purpose. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-delta) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-discover**: Double-Diamond discovery intake for a new idea in the standard or regulated lane. Use when the idea is vague, the user says "I want to build" or "new feature", or `arsdlc next` reports DISCOVER. Runs JTBD, workaround mining, 5 Whys, How-Might-We, MoSCoW and the riskiest assumption with the arsdlc-grill interview discipline, seeds docs/glossary.md, writes docs/brief.md and seed EARS requirements. Ends in a verdict - build, spike first, defer, or do not build - so a bad idea dies cheaply. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-discover) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-flow**: The arsdlc router. Use at the start of ANY engineering task in a repo with an arsdlc.json or docs/features/ directory, when the user says "next", "where are we", "what should I do", "continue", "status", or before any stage work. Reads state from disk via `npx arsdlc next` (or from the files by hand when the CLI is unavailable), respects the feature's lane, reads any handoff, then hands off to exactly one arsdlc-* skill. Never guesses state from chat history. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-flow) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-grill**: Relentless structured interview that turns a vague plan, idea or spec into shared understanding before anything is built. Use when the user says "grill me", "interview me", "poke holes", "stress-test this", "what am I missing", or when discover, spec, plan, delta or retro needs a human decision. Works the design tree in rounds, gives a recommended answer with every question, finds facts itself instead of asking, writes settled decisions to disk. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-grill) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-handoff**: Compact the session into a handoff document so a fresh session, another agent, or a human continues without loss. Use when the user says "handoff", "save progress", "I need to stop", "context is getting long", "continue this tomorrow", before /compact or /clear, when the window is over ~70% full, or at the end of a stage that took more than one session. Writes handoff.md in the feature folder; arsdlc-flow reads it next start. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-handoff) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-implement**: Implement one task at a time, test-first at the agreed seam, with the ponytail ladder, recorded evidence and zero scope creep. Use when `arsdlc next` reports BUILD, or the user says "implement", "build T3", "write the code", "continue", "tdd". Never edits the spec silently, intent changes go through arsdlc-delta. Never claims tests pass without an evidence record. Routes fix tasks to arsdlc-debug. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-implement) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-plan**: Produce plan.md - architecture, constitution check, explicit decisions with alternatives and ladder rung, data model, interface contracts, research that resolves every unknown, seams, tracer-bullet breakdown, smoke path, risks, and a Traceability table mapping every requirement to tasks - then ask a human for G1. Use when `arsdlc next` reports PLAN, the user says "plan this", "architecture", "how should we build it", or a spec just got signed. The agent never approves its own plan. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-plan) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-ponytail**: Bridge to Ponytail, the lazy-senior-developer discipline that forces the simplest solution that actually works (YAGNI, stdlib before custom code, native platform before dependencies, one line before fifty). Use on every coding task - plan decisions, implement, review - when the user says "ponytail", "be lazy", "simplest", "minimal", "yagni", "do less", or complains about bloat. Prefers the official ponytail skill when installed, and names what you may never be lazy about. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-ponytail) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-quality**: Bridge to code-quality-tools, the runner guide for lint, autofix, dead-code, complexity and security CLIs on JS/TS and Python (fallow, eslint, biome, knip, tsc, ruff, mypy, vulture, bandit, semgrep - which tool, in what order, what mutates vs detects). Use during BUILD and VERIFY for a mechanical pass before G3 or a security scan, or when the user says "lint", "dead code", "typecheck", "complexity". Tools advise, the CLI gates. ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-quality) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-release**: Prepare and gate a release - rollout.md with strategy and rollback (G5, human approved), then guardrails.yaml and runbook.md for runtime budgets, kill switch and on-call actions (G6). ([AnshRoshan/arsdlc](https://github.com/AnshRoshan/arsdlc/tree/HEAD/skills/arsdlc-release) · 2 copies)
- **arsdlc-retro**: Close the loop on a finished feature, incident or sprint so the process gets better instead of just longer. Compares what the spec promised with what shipped, which gate caught what, where time went, what the agent over-built, and turns each lesson into a concrete edit to the constitution, taste, glossary or a skill. - **arsdlc-review**: Independent reviewer for the acceptance gate G4.
